a 2

## TO ALL
*Who love true*
# PIETY
*and*
# PATRIOTIC FREEDOM

Wishes prosperity and salvation

->JACOBUS VERHEIDEN.<-

<image-desc>A large ornamental historiated drop cap 'D' depicting a muscular figure wrestling with a lion, set within a landscape border of flowers and scrolling leaves.</image-desc>
The highest of all virtues is piety <note>The Dutch *Godt-vruchticheyt* implies both a fear of God and a life that bears spiritual fruit</note>: a good beyond all value is freedom. The latter <note>freedom</note> brings exceedingly great fruits in this present life, the former <note>piety</note> in the life to come. So that I may speak here of the goodness of piety and freedom among those who love these things, reason demands it, the current subject requires it, and the circumstances and the times also urge and desire it. Piety unites us with God; freedom unites us with Nature. Piety is above all pleasing to God; freedom makes a pleasant and lovely assembly—

<summary>Jacobus Verheiden opens his preface by praising piety and freedom as the two most essential virtues, framing them as necessary for both spiritual salvation and the preservation of the Dutch fatherland.</summary>

# P R E F A C E

society of men, and maintains it. Since man is made by Nature not only to exercise Reason, but also for Society <note>The original Dutch *Versamelinge* refers to a gathering or social assembly, here used in the sense of a structured civil society</note>; what, tell me, can be as firmly bound to Nature as this Freedom? For this reason, we shall first speak briefly of Piety, and thereafter of Freedom, as well as those things that conflict with both, and finally of the Preservation of both and their Harmony, without which no Republic or Society can endure or exist. All of this we shall relate fittingly, as it serves our purpose: so that the lovers of true Piety and Patriotic Freedom may understand what sincere cause I have for dedicating this work of mine to them.

The Foundation of all virtues, then, is Piety; if this is taken away, it is necessary that all Loyalty and the society of men, as well as that preeminent virtue, Justice—as Cicero <note>Marcus Tullius Cicero (106–43 BCE), the Roman statesman and philosopher often cited by Renaissance humanists</note> writes—be taken away as well. 

I do not speak here of the Piety or Religion of the Heathens, namely the Egyptians, Greeks, Romans, and others: nor do I wish to remark here on what has been written by Hermes Trismegistus <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure associated with a syncretic blend of Greek and Egyptian philosophy</note>, Plato, Cicero, and many others, who were all "Christians without Christ"—that is, those who in some ways were not far from the understanding that Christians have of God. Although they spoke of God in their own manner, they nevertheless did not please God. For no one can please God without God. No one has God without—

<summary>Verheiden argues that piety is the essential foundation of society and justice, claiming that while ancient pagan philosophers had some glimmers of truth, true piety and freedom are only possible through a proper relationship with God.</summary>

-># P R E F A C E .<-

without Christ being known. Therefore, those people wander very foolishly who, speaking impressively of Divine matters according to their human understanding, seem to place the Heathens <note>original: "Heydenen"; referring to non-Christian Greeks and Romans</note> among the number of the godly. That is the true Religion <term>Religie: from the Latin 'religare', meaning to bind or tie back</term>, which binds us to G O D through C H R I S T: from which Christ, as our Head and Leader, we are called Christians. From where else is this true Religion and Faith to be drawn than from G O D’S  W O R D? Where else is it to be found than with Him who has clearly spoken there, S E A R C H  T H E  S C R I P T U R E S <note>A reference to John 5:39</note>: he who strives against this shows by his deeds that he is no Christian: and with such a person (as one who rejects or does not know the foundations or principles) it is not fitting to dispute, as has been said of old. For the Scripture is like a Rule <note>original: "Recht-snoer"; literally a plumb line or measuring cord used by builders</note>, according to which everything concerning Religion must be aligned. The Scripture is the Touchstone <note>A stone used to test the purity of gold and silver</note>, which makes known and rejects everything that is false. This rejects human understanding, reasons, wisdom, and all decorated and self-willed worships, which we commonly call Superstitions or by-belief, all of which proceed from the ingenuity of men. Thus far concerning Religion or Godliness. Now it remains to propose something here concerning Freedom. Nothing agrees so much with Nature as Freedom, which is also given to the mute animals by Nature. This is desired by the evil as well as the good, the faint-hearted and the unkind along with the courageous: who...

<meta>This page continues the preface, likely following an introduction to the author's philosophical or political framework regarding the nature of liberty and law.</meta>

# ->P R E F A C E .<-

*no one who is pious loses it, except with his own life, just as the wise and excellent historian Sallust <note>Gaius Sallustius Crispus (86–35 BC), a Roman historian known for his critiques of moral decline</note> writes, who is a brave and stern rebuker of vices. According to this Freedom, we live as we wish, unless Reason or Law, and also Decency, desire otherwise. We are also forbidden by force from living according to Freedom. But of the Freedom that is subjected to violence, as is well known, nothing is spoken here. True Freedom, namely that which is armed with Laws, is loved and embraced by the good. To which Laws so much is to be attributed, that those are rightly considered free who, in the midst of their Freedom, acknowledge and profess to live according to the Laws. So that Freedom is to serve the Laws. It is therefore necessary that Freedom stands upon the firm ground of Laws and Virtues. This is briefly recounted concerning the very great good of life-awakening Piety <term>Godtvruchticheyt: a blend of religious devotion and moral uprightness</term> and Freedom. Which, if everyone together understood, embraced, and accepted with reverence, and also honored and maintained with good manners; truly everything in this unstable World would be better and more steadfastly established. But since Good (as the ancient Sages have said) dwells near to Evil: and every Good, the more glorious it is, the more heavily it is attacked by enemies; so we wish to consider what and of what kind the enemies are, who attack Piety and Freedom, and labor to bring these down. There are then many kinds of enemies. Some with weapons openly,*

<meta>catchword: some-</meta>

<summary>This preface defines true freedom not as license, but as a life governed by law, reason, and virtue, citing the Roman historian Sallust. It warns that such noble freedom is constantly under attack by various enemies of piety and order.</summary>
<keywords>Freedom, Law, Sallust, Piety, Virtue, Ethics, Political Philosophy</keywords>

# -> PREFACE <-

some craftily oppose these with deceitful practices: on the other hand, there are others who, being ignorant and unwise, fight against godliness and liberty through their lives and manners, while claiming to stand for godliness and liberty. Tyrants are open enemies: those who, when they oppose Religion and Liberty, fight against Heaven and Nature; they are rightly said to fight against God. A Tyrant is such a creature that is most hateful to God and men. The world has always produced such untamed and monstrous animals in heaps, and today still produces and maintains them. Examples are not necessary here. I consider that today the Spaniard <note>The author refers to the Spanish Habsburg rule during the Eighty Years' War</note> sufficiently presents himself to us as that most grasping and ambitious animal: of whose terrible Greed and Pride (which bring forth that Tyranny) History and both Worlds <note>Referring to the Old World of Europe and the New World of the Americas</note> let us hear: and also let the Writers, Kings, Princes, and Republics of future times speak, when that Spanish Tyranny—an Empire obtained through evil practices—shall begin to collapse. What a terrifying, Hellish enemy the Pope is, we know well enough: who promotes and supports not only his Antichristian Religion, but also the Ambition of the Spaniard and others, out of hatred for the true Christian Religion: he who has a breast full of knavery and is a master of a thousand deceits. Of whose godlessness and villainies it is better to say nothing than to say little. There are now also very many who boast of their great love—

# PREFACE

to be lovers of Religion and Liberty, but *by their way of life and the practice of virtues, they do not make this known.* Truly, we err greatly if we allow ourselves to believe that true Religion and the Liberty of our Fatherland can stand firm and long-lasting without the practice of the Virtues, which the rules and laws of both set before us. Religion is most supported and preserved by Piety, and Liberty by Virtue. Virtues are increased, strengthened, and protected by Virtues. Just as Virtues are obtained through Virtues, so it is also necessary that the same are lost through Vices <note>original: "Ghebreecken," referring to moral failings or defects of character</note>. But what shall I say of Vices? These can much more easily be accused than improved: unless someone wishes to have this world of ours—in which the craving for Vices takes the upper hand—entirely and completely changed. To this point now (God help us) have the being and manners of Europe or Christendom come, that brave, wise, and upright men complain that the old Discipline <term>Discipline: refers to the rigorous moral and social order of previous generations</term> and manners have decayed: that the exercises of Piety and the zeal for Virtue are no longer as abundant and vigilant as they used to be. Behold the Kingdoms, Dominions, and Republics of Europe: in which the old and laudable manners of our ancestors are changed, or change daily more and more; upon which Old Manners the Ancients spoke with all truth that the Republic, or the Government of the Land and Cities, stands firmly. # PREFACE.

If one were to ask the *Italian*, the *German*, the *Frenchman*, the *Englishman*, the *Netherlander*, and many others: they would testify to the same. I mean among these nations those men who are wise, and who have made virtue their own. For the common people often consider those things to be virtues which wise and upright men hold to be defects. Many learned and wise men testify to this through their sentiments and writings: among whom is *Ogier Ghiselin de Busbecq* <note>original: "Augerius Busbequius" (1522–1592), a Flemish diplomat and herbalist</note>, a man whom we, because of his singular wisdom and experience, may rightly call the *Commines* <note>original: "Cominæum"; referring to Philippe de Commines, the famous chronicler of statecraft</note> of our time, even as he far surpassed *Commines* in terms of learning. This man writes vehemently against the corrupted manners of *Europe*, and does so in writings wherein the excellence of his judgment is evident. So too *Hotman* <note>original: "Hotomannus"; François Hotman (1524–1590), a French legal scholar</note>, a very learned man in many excellent things, likewise a very upright and courageous Jurist <term>Jurist: an expert in law and legal theory</term>, who speaks manfully against the corrupted manners and defects of his *Frenchmen*. In like manner does *Friedrich Tilemann* <note>original: "Fredericus Tilemannus"</note>, a man experienced in Law and History, who, being a *German*, does not express the best opinion of the manners of his *Germany*, nor of *Europe*, in his *Political Disputation on the Downfall of Republics*: whose judgment, learning, and wisdom, being drawn from the very heart of History, show themselves to be extraordinary. So also the *Englishman*, the *Pole*, and others more (I mean excellent men of the

# PREFACE

*lovers of ancient ways) greatly despise the newly-corrupted customs of their Fatherland. But what shall we say now of the Swiss, whose Republic is praised above all, and who are held to be brave defenders of their Freedom, as well as earnest pursuers of the older ways? We still remember, when we dwelled among them, that we heard excellent, learned, and wise men judge poorly of certain new or foreign Swiss customs, creeping into the Republic now and then. And Zwingli <note>Huldrych Zwingli (1484–1531), a leader of the Reformation in Switzerland</note> was in his time greatly disturbed, not enduring these same fashions: so he also frequently and diligently exhorted his people to the adoption of the old ancestral customs and usages. What Josias Simler <note>Josias Simler (1530–1576), a Swiss theologian and classicist famous for his work "De Republica Helvetiorum"</note> wrote about thirty years ago concerning the change in his Fatherland's customs, in the Preface to his books which he published on the Swiss Republic, we may understand from his Oration of Praise <note>original: "Lof-spreuck"</note> included here in this book on Folio 129. Which I, for the sake of those who love Freedom, to whom these Orations of Praise are also dedicated, have wished to add here. What is there now to judge concerning the Netherlanders? I mean especially those who are free there. That these manners are easier to criticize (as has been said) than to improve at this time, shall be judged by pious and wise men. This is presented here by one who is a lover of true Piety and the Freedom of the Fatherland.*

# PREFACE.

having. What shall I say now concerning Religion? If Liberty is not maintained by its virtues, and if evil habits continue their progress, how shall we follow those ways which Religion requires? I find that to be true which is in the mouths and in the books of some, and which proceeded from a man (a human being, I should say) of great renown, namely: *This present Age does not please me enough, in which we see (I shall make bold to say it) that there has been no time more pregnant with Religions, and less fruitful in Godliness.* <note>The author is likely quoting a contemporary humanist criticising the sectarian strife of the Reformation era.</note> Truly, right Religion demands Godliness; and Godliness demands Virtues: through Virtues, Religion and Liberty must be maintained and preserved. Those who withdraw Liberty from Religion, and Religion from Liberty, act wrongly. Those also speak wrongly who call every man's opinion "Religion"; likewise those who honor License, or the improper will to act, with the name of "Liberty" or "Freedom." We have now (alas!) come to those times in which evil things are given good names, and good things are given evil names. There is but one true Religion, which is contained in the Holy Scripture. Liberty is enclosed within the Right of the Laws. Now I turn myself to the Netherlanders who love Religion and Liberty, to whom I, as a Netherlander, have dedicated this Work.

<summary>The author argues that true liberty and religion are inseparable and must be rooted in virtue and law rather than personal opinion or license. He dedicates the work to his fellow Netherlanders who value these principles.</summary>

# PREFACE

->Consider here for a little while, and accept what is worthy of your thought.<-

Our Enemy <note>The author refers to King Philip II of Spain, though he avoids naming him directly to emphasize his villainous role.</note> (it is not necessary that I name him, as he is well enough known) who has been fighting against our Freedom for so many years now—by what right has he sought to deprive us of our Freedom? Was it not under the cover of *Religion*? By what right does he conquer and miserably torment the *New World* <note>The Americas</note>? Under the cover of *Religion*, through the *Liberality* <note>The granting of lands</note> of the most powerful *Pope Alexander VI Borgia*, one of his own, having been a *Spaniard*: who is regarded not as a man, but as a *monster*, just as the *Historians* testify to us. Likewise, he has labored marvelously, and still does, to take over *England*, *France*, and other *Kingdoms*, being armed with his *Religion*, standing upon the powerful *Bull* <note>A formal papal decree</note> of the *Roman Bull-man* <note>A pun on the Papal Bull and a "bully" or executioner</note>. Therefore, he hides under the cover of *Religion* his *Greed, Ambition*, and his *Tyranny* which arises from these aforementioned vices. Just as in former times the proud *Xerxes* <note>The Persian King</note> sheltered himself against the heat of the *Sun* under the shadow of the great, beautiful *Plane Tree* <note>original: "Platani"</note> spreading its branches wide: so also does this man cover the heat of his burning *Ambition* under the shadow of *Religion*. In order that he might pull us under his yoke, he has left nothing untried that might serve him. Force and schemes have not been lacking there. Sometimes he has undertaken to subjugate us with weapons: sometimes to draw us to his will through *Discord* or *Dissension* <note>original: "Discordie ofte Tweedracht"</note>. Again he has

# PREFACE.

...found it good to lure us to him through a false Peace: again through treacheries, clever deceit, and the enticement of some, he has intended to carry out his affairs. Often he has also sought to take away the heads of the Government. He has also deemed it useful to his cause to exclude the Netherlandish Merchants from the Harbors of the kingdom of Spain. But what more? He has also found it good for himself to drive out Religion with Religion. Let us pay attention to this. For herein our enemies seem to labor bravely. Just as the Sicilian Tyrant Dionysius <note>Dionysius I of Syracuse, often cited as the archetype of a cruel and manipulative ruler</note>, so also our Spaniard has his Sophists, whom we call *Ignatianos* <note>original: "Ignatianos"; followers of Ignatius of Loyola</note> or Jesuits, who set fires all around: who through their number, arts, and sciences, which they misuse, possess great power. They write, they trot, they run, spying out all places far and wide: now with sweet-talk, now with threats they persist. That which is at rest they stir into unrest. These things move us so that we embrace and keep whole our Religion, not only as Religion—that is, for the sake of God’s honor—but also through the love of Freedom. Therefore, that which we present here was some years ago not only rightly conceived, but also wittily expressed by a Medal <term>Penning: a commemorative coin or medal often used for political propaganda</term> in the Netherlands. On one side of this Medal one sees two Hands firmly grasping each other, having between them arrows held and pressed tightly together, with this Inscription, **WITH GOD'S HELP** <note>original Latin: "DEO IVVANTE"</note>: On the other side stands a Column, where

# PREFACE.

*The base or foundation is a Book, specifically named Religion. At the top of this Column is placed a Hat, as a sign of Liberty.* This Column is held by the hands of the Lords States of the United Netherlandish Provinces, with this inscription: <term>HANC TVEMVR: HAC NITIMVR</term> <note>Latin: "This we protect: On this we lean"</note>, that is, WE PROTECT LIBERTY: ON RELIGION WE LEAN. *Both sides of this medal express this meaning: We together, harmoniously leaning upon Religion, preserve our Liberty.*

Very well. *For without a Foundation the Republic cannot exist. Let Religious Liberty then stand firm, and Religion remain free; protected, that is, and being free from those things which might be a hindrance to both. Let us then now not only avoid the Spanish Sophists* <note>A reference to Jesuit or Catholic theologians who used complex logic to argue against Dutch independence</note>, *but also others, among whom are those who (I shudder to say) occupy themselves in fashioning new, strangely-derived Christs, in mocking Christ's Martyrs, and in establishing religion through simulation and dissimulation with godlessness: those who, perhaps hoping for some confusion, believe that they will only then be in order. But here I will restrain myself.*

It is for this reason that I have wished to dedicate these likenesses or life-like engraved figures of some excellent Theological men—or those especially experienced in God's Word—whom we have been able to obtain, with their accompanying eulogies, above all to you Netherlanders (for whom this is most intended). These are the ones who the ROMAN

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page concludes the preface, following the previous discussion of historical figures who opposed ecclesiastical corruption. The tone is polemical and typical of early 17th-century Protestant literature.</meta>

# ->PREFACE.<-

*...have fought against the* **ANTICHRIST** *with great earnestness. This is the Master of Tempests or Storms, who sits there like a* Jupiter <note>The king of the Roman gods, used here to characterize the Pope's perceived arrogance.</note> *on his* Capitoline *hill* <note>original: "Campidolischen berg"; referring to the Capitoline Hill in Rome, the seat of ancient Roman power and later associated with Papal authority.</note>, *moving everything with his thunder and lightning, tearing Kings and Princes away from one another, locking and commanding throughout the world whatever he desires, suffering no one on this earth to judge him. With these men, then, let us delight our eyes and minds for a while: likewise heartily praying to* **God**, *that the Kingdom of* **Christ**, *His Son, our only Mediator and Savior, may be daily more and more established, and that the* **Antichrist's** <note>A common Reformation-era term for the Papacy.</note>, *as well as all tyrants', downfall and shame may come to pass: That the Ministry of the Divine Word and of the Schools be maintained, from which may come forth those who may, in due time, protect and preserve Religion and the* **Freedom of the Fatherland** *against the tyrants of souls and bodies.*

The 20th of July, in the year after the Birth of our Lord
Jesus Christ, 1602.

...obtained. Some are also seen here which have now for the first time been engraved by the hand of **Hondius** <note>Hendrick Hondius I (1573–1650), a prominent Dutch engraver and publisher</note> and brought into the light, for the pleasure of the **Godly Beholder**. Our **Deipnosophists** <term>Deipnosophists: literally 'dinner-table philosophers,' a reference to Athenaeus's work, here used to mock those who prefer comfortable intellectualizing over religious conviction</term> or **Foreign Table-Sages** do otherwise nowadays; they who seem unwilling to endure the *Effigies* or *Likenesses* of those at whose very name **Rome** herself is terrified.

But let these good men rather learn what the **Pope** is about: he who in all places attempts the utmost against us all. It is not necessary, **Godly Beholder**, that I set before you the proper use of these **Figures** or **Likenesses**. The followers of the Antichrist, whose eyes are clouded by **Superstition**, act differently with their paintings, statues, and small images, which they possess in great heaps. Look upon these here in such a way that you also look up toward **Heaven**, in which those who have fought against the **Antichrist** live in eternal joys.

# TO THE GOOD-
## willed Reader.

We have not always followed the order of Time throughout. # TO THE UPRIGHT
## OBSERVER.

<image-desc>Decorative floral woodcut drop cap 'D' at the beginning of the text.</image-desc>
That we have brought forth certain things in some of these *Eulogies* <note>original: "Lof-spreucken"; formal speeches or writings of praise</note> from the writings of those whom we praise—and that this is not entirely customary—you must understand has not been done without reason. For the *Authors* <note>original: "Scribenten"</note>, when they speak for themselves, are considered to speak better than their commentators or those who proclaim their praise. Furthermore, we have only done so when the matter and the *History* <note>original: "Historie"</note> required it and when it was beneficial for our own times. Now, as for our frequent use of words and phrases taken from the conduct of war <note>original: "Krijgs-handel"</note>; let no one find fault with this. For we speak of those who have fought against the *Roman Antichrist* <note>original: "Roomschen Antichrist"; a common Reformation-era term for the Pope</note>, who is entirely occupied in maintaining his affairs not only with *Papal Bulls* <note>original: "Bul-brieven"; formal proclamations issued by the Pope</note> and deceptions, but also with wars. Who can object, then, if the sentiments of the combatants and of those who stubbornly resist are appropriately expressed through these *Military metaphors* <note>original: "Krijgh-spreucken"</note>? Furthermore, someone might wonder why the *Spaniard* is so often attacked in this work. But such a person must consider that won-

without which the *Pope* and the *Spaniard* <note>Referring to King Philip II of Spain and the Papacy, the two major powers of the Counter-Reformation</note> surely agree: without which I do not know if the *Pope* would be able to keep his *Seven-hilled Rome*. This is this *Spanish* son who, through his *Catholic* universal right, takes over the World: which World, as he says, recognizes the *Pope* as its Father according to *Religion*, but him according to *Dominion* <note>original: "Dominie"; referring to temporal or political rule</note>. He shall, moreover, also be mindful that this *Work* of ours is dedicated to the true *Lovers of Godliness* and *Patriotic Freedom*: to both of which the *Spaniard* is a very heavy enemy. Finally, he must also think that the one who publishes this is one from the number of the Freedom-loving Netherlanders, with whom the *Spaniard* has made no such treaty that one may not speak that which the Truth itself is crying out aloud. Farewell. Furthermore, correct several *Errors* or *Faults* <note>original: "Erraten ofte Fauten"</note> above all: which the end of this Book is pointing out.

# SONNET.

See here, beloved spectators, depicted after the Life
*The Godly Men, who like Paul were chosen* <note>Referring to the Apostle Paul, often cited as the model for divine calling.</note>
As the Trumpets of God’s Mouth, upon Zion’s watchtower
*They are driven by* **THE LORD’S SPIRIT**, *to warn us.*
*So that from* **BABYLON** <note>In this context, Babylon represents a state of sin, confusion, or the perceived corruption of the established Church from which the faithful must flee.</note> *we might depart, and live*
According to **GOD’S WORD**, like newborn children
*In* **LOVE** *zealous, far from hatred and wrath.*
Therefore, read well through the short account described here.
*Then you shall truly find them to be no mere laymen,*
Skinners or fleecers, as has been said (out of hatred):
*But Men as learned and pious as were ever found.*
Yet not all are shown here who wrought such great work:
*But those we had at hand and quickly considered,*
Though many more such **MEN** we could have brought forth.

# BERENGARIUS,
## ARCHDEACON OF ANGERS.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'A' featuring foliate designs and scrollwork.</image-desc>

Behold here the renowned Berengarius. Hold him in high esteem, and marvel at such a man, who, gazing toward Heaven and pointing to the same with a refined hand, seems to indicate the place to which *Christ* bodily, in the sight of his Apostles, was truly taken up and ascended: from whence he shall come in like manner as they saw him ascending into Heaven. Mark this man, I say, who stands here as if upon the threshold of this Holy Gallery, in which are beheld (drawn from life) the Likenesses of those who sought and taught Heavenly things: who preached the spiritual Kingdom of *Christ*, and with all diligence fought against that Kingdom of *Antichrist* <note>A common Reformation-era term for the Papacy</note>. This is that *Berengarius, Archdeacon of Angers, a Frenchman*, who was a very fierce opponent of the natural or fleshly presence of *Christ* in the Supper of the Lord: who, being well-versed in the Holy Scripture and a diligent searcher of the writings of the Early Church Fathers, taught with a brave spirit and the zeal of his mind that, in the *Sacrament of the Lord's Supper*, *Christ* is not in the Bread in a natural manner, but after the manner and nature of a Sacrament, namely, as in a sign. Wherefore he, not suffering the fleshly presence of *Christ* in the Holy Supper, seemed to set the entire Kingdom of the Roman Pope (who seeks there all that is fleshly and earthly) into a total uproar. For this man's teaching, being confirmed by the Holy Scripture and by the authority and testimonies of the upright Early Church Fathers, was spread far and wide through Italy, Germany, France, and other lands, around the year of Christ's Ascension 1020 <note>The text likely refers to the year 1050, the height of the controversy</note>. Therefore, a Council was called together by Pope *Leo IX* at *Vercelli* (a city situated at the entrance of Italy near Piedmont): where the teaching of *Berengarius* concerning the *Sacrament* of the Supper (in which he would not have the Bread worshipped in the place of God) was condemned. Likewise, some years thereafter, a Council was gathered within Rome by

<meta>catchword: Nico- (referring to Pope Nicholas II)</meta>

-> B E R E N G A R I V S <-

To Nicholas II, who was crowned Pope in the year of our Lord Christ 1057. To which Council *Berengarius* was summoned: where—through the terrifying threats of the Pope—he (see here the weakness of men) recanted his teaching. At which the Pope was wonderfully pleased, and sent the Recantation to the cities of Italy, Germany, France, and many other lands: as is evident from that most well-known chapter of the Popes' *Decrees*, *Ego Berengarius. de Consecrat. Distinct. II.* <note>Latin: "I, Berengarius." A famous canon law text where Berengarius was forced to admit to a physical Real Presence in the Eucharist.</note> In which these words stand, full of hideous blasphemy, which were read there according to the agreement, judgment, and prescription of the Council, namely: *I believe that the Body of our Lord JESUS CHRIST is sensibly (sensualiter) and in truth handled and broken by the hands of the Mass-Priests, and crushed into pieces by the teeth of the faithful.* Which words the blunt Popish *Gloss* <note>The "Glossa Ordinaria," a standard commentary on canon law.</note> noting and rebuking, warned the Reader in this manner: *Unless you understand these words of BERENGARIUS quite rightly, you shall fall into greater heresy than BERENGARIUS ever advocated.* Furthermore, the Papists may not accuse your teaching, *Berengarius*, of novelty, for it is that which you protect with the word of God (which is the oldest teaching), and confirm with the ancient Fathers, especially with *Augustine's* writings and sentiments. Which is also very clearly understood from the opposition of *Lanfranc*, who was a very harsh adversary to you. This *Lanfranc*, beloved observer and Reader, was from *Lombardy*, born in *Pavia*, being a guest who was cunning and quick of wit, who at that time was very renowned for his Sophistical tricks, all kinds of subtleties and little arts: from which the *Scholastic Theology*, that is, the Popes' school of sharp-wittedness in Divine things, was called *Lanfranciana*: by which, at that time, the *Theology of Augustine and Jerome* was not much esteemed. This *Lanfranc*, puffed up by a mixture of subtlety and ambition, and being fashioned according to the Pope's brain, fought knightly for the *Carnal Presence* as if for a *Roman Helen* <note>A reference to Helen of Troy; implying the doctrine of the Real Presence was a "prize" or cause for war.</note> against *Berengarius*. Which champion, through his brave services, was made Archbishop of *Canterbury* in England: for whom also *Alexander II*, successor to *Nicholas II*, rose with reverence (which is something new for a Pope): and that through the love of the beautiful future bride *Transubstantiation*, which, being yet without a name, was [clothed] by *Lanfranc* through the

the cosmetic of his fine Sophistry now seemed to have given it some color. Previously, there were also *Johannes Scotus* <note>John Scottus Eriugena (c. 800–877), an Irish theologian</note>, a man of extraordinary learning, a disciple of *Bede*, a fellow-teacher of *Alcuin*, and *Bertram* <note>Ratramnus of Corbie (died c. 868), author of 'De corpore et sanguine Domini'</note>, who at that time held a great reputation for his knowledge and experience in the Holy Scripture: who wrote against this bodily or Fleshly Presence, as it was not yet well known at that time: and their judgment was generally accepted, without there being any who publicly punished them. And that happened two hundred years or more before *Berengarius* was born. Even today, the book of *Bertram* is found in the hands of many, having been frequently printed, written to *Charles the Great* <note>Charlemagne; though the author likely means Charles the Bald, to whom Ratramnus actually wrote</note>: by whose command he made this same book, and also dedicated or assigned it to the Emperor *Charles*. Both of these, namely *Scotus* and *Bertram*, were very highly regarded by *Charles the Great* for their learning and godliness. But so that the Pope might better maintain his Bread-God <note>A polemical term for the consecrated host in the Eucharist</note>, *Transubstantiation*—that is, the Change of Substance, or the transferring of one Being into another—having been born at *Rome* about a hundred years after *Berengarius'* death, also spread through the Roman world. This also another *Johannes Scotus*, surnamed *Duns* <note>Duns Scotus (c. 1266–1308)</note>, a disciple of *Alexander of Hales* (which *Scotus* is commonly called the *Subtle Doctor*, who died in the year of our Lord 1308) seems to admit, writing on the fourth book of the *Sentences* of *Peter Lombard* <note>The standard medieval textbook of theology</note>, which is like a whetstone for the understandings of the Popish School-Theologians. Likewise, there have been many other Popish Doctors who, not understanding the manner of this *Transubstantiation* or Change of Substance, and finding the same to be estranged from the Holy Scripture, have taken their refuge, as if to a sheet-anchor, in the Roman Church as the mother of this fine daughter. Again, there are many others who forge now this, now that manner of *Transubstantiation*, and are at odds with one another in this labor. So great is the labor and strife of the Roman Cyclopes in fighting for this God-and-man-patchwork. Which is also no wonder. For this *Transubstantiation* (since it is the soul of the *Mass*, which *Mass* is a short summary of the Popish Religion) the Popes have thought it good to maintain and confirm with all their might, in all sorts of ways—

# BERENGARIUS

to decorate and to worship. In order that this *Transubstantiation* <note>The doctrine that the bread and wine become the actual body and blood of Christ.</note> should receive its full honor and reverence, Pope *Urban* IV, in the year of our Lord 1264, instituted the Feast of the *Sacrament*, or the Bread-God (which they call the Body of Christ). This day was to be held as a solemn day on the fourth day after the Feast of the Trinity: honoring the Bread-God through processions, candles, torches, costly canopies <note>original: "verhemelde costelijcke tenten"</note>, banners, solemn Masses, and Octaves. Furthermore, he proclaimed great Indulgences, so that this Roman God might be served and honored above all gods. Indeed, he finally gave orders to *Thomas Aquinas* to scrape together some things from the figures and shadows of the Old Testament in honor of this Feast; which *Thomas* has been regarded as a foster-father of *Transubstantiation*. Which Feast the succeeding Popes, and especially in our times, have so honored and do honor, that by this idolatry—being seen as complete—it is understood that no greater idolatry could be added to it. I shall perhaps be rebuked by some for relating too many things here (I who should be speaking of the praise of *Berengarius*); but so that the diligence and very useful teaching of *Berengarius* may be better noted, I have presented the *Mass* as a short summary of all heresies, which receives its spirit or life from *Transubstantiation*.

Let the wretched and abominable Mass-priests curse you then, *Berengarius*, you who have *Christ* with you, sitting in Heaven at the right hand of His Father: you who have had repentance for the recantation to which you were forced by terrible threats, and have left behind an example for standing up for the Truth of Christ.

Furthermore, Viewer, know that we have obtained this *Effigy* or likeness of *Berengarius* through the favor and love of Master *Francis van Molin*, Dean of *St. Savior's Church* at *Blois* in *France*, being a learned man and experienced in antiquities. He says he found this image, which you see here, in the year of our Lord 1568 in a very old book resting in the Library of *St. Martin of Tours*: with the addition of these verses, which make known to the viewer and reader the singular honor and praise of *Berengarius*. Which read as follows:

*He who now makes the world wonder shall do so forever,*
*That* B E R E N G A R I U S, *who suffers an immortal death.* <note>A poetic paradox suggesting his fame outlives his mortal life, despite his condemnation.</note>
*He who trod the heavy path of Faith until the end,*
*On January's first day was snatched from us to our sorrow:*
*That day, cursed day, and the faithless World,*
*Which wickedly destroys the ornament and form of all things,*
*Which laid low the spiritual Estate, virtue, hope, and glory,*
*Destroying the Plumb-line of Justice, and Justice itself,*
*Whatever the Philosophers or Poets ever sang*
*Vanishes through his Spirit, and the sweet eloquence of his tongue.*
*Whom the mourning-woven cloth covers: and beyond the need of thirst,*
*Never sought drink, and without hunger, neither food nor bread.*
*When God gave us this pious, chosen man,*
*He spoke: "As others die, this one is born to me."*

# B E R E N G A R I U S,
### Archdeacon of Angers.

Why do you lift your hand and look toward the eye of the Firmament? <note>The "eye of the firmament" refers to the heavens or the sun.</note>
Tell us, Berengarius? What is your meaning in this?
I teach that Christ's body is in the Heaven high above.
Far then from the Mass is Christ's true Being. <note>Berengarius was famous for opposing the doctrine of Transubstantiation, arguing for a spiritual rather than physical presence.</note>

B E R E N G A R I U S is given to the Christian,
Whom he truly preached and taught to know Christ.
And denied that C H R I S T' S body lives in the Bread.
As no food for the belly: but honored Christ's body as food for the Spirit.

-># REGISTER OF THE BOOKS OF BERENGARIUS.<-

# On the horrible Transubstantiation or the
## changing of the bread into the flesh and blood of Christ.

> Words odd in number, mumbled and shrouded in mystery,
> The Mass-priest uses like magic to conjure Christ together;
> But just as his Monster transforms nature,
> So too can such madness find no place in my verses.

*Regarding a large Catalog or Register of the Books made by Berengarius, we cannot recount anything extraordinary:*

*It is well to consider that he, being well-learned and eloquent, must have written many diverse books: of which this one is known:*

->**On the Body and Blood of the Lord.**<-

*In which Book he (writing against the fleshly presence of Christ in the Lord's Supper <note>original: "Avontmael"</note>) makes it known that he is well-practiced in the Holy Scripture and the books of the ancient Fathers: just as can be well-discerned from the counter-writings of Lanfranc <note>Lanfranc of Bec, a contemporary and theological opponent of Berengarius</note>.*

*Berengarius, now having repentance for the recantation of his Teachings which occurred in the Roman Council, has also written*

->**Against the Roman Council, and namely against Cardi-**<-

<column-break/>

**-nal HUMBERT;** *by whose prescription the Form of the recantation in the Council was drafted.*

*In the Book "On the Body and Blood of the Lord," he writes thus of the Anti-Christian Church:*

> Truly, he says, Truth has not perceived that the Church is holy, but rather that it is the Church of the wicked, and a dwelling-place of Satan.

*Which is also testified by* **Cardinal Benno**, *Arch-Priest of the Roman Church, who (in the life of Pope* **Hildebrand**, *later named* **Gregory VII**, *an ambitious Monster, by whom it is thought that* **Nicholas II** *was poisoned) writes thus of that same time:* The Religion of the Clergy at that time brought forth nothing other than treachery and greed.

# JOHN WYCLIFFE
## ENGLISHMAN.

<image-desc>A large ornamental drop cap 'H' depicting a scholar seated at a desk with an open book, surrounded by decorative floral and architectural elements.</image-desc>Here is this WYCLIFFE presented, as the one who, among all learned, godly, and chosen men of former times, first uncovered the mysteries of the Kingdom of wickedness and deceit in England. Which Island and Kingdom, flourishing above others, has brought forth for us many kinds of men rich in the Arts, and brave and experienced in the Word of God, who—both by preaching and writing—contradicted, opposed, and rejected the misunderstandings, blindness, and corrupt customs (which had greatly increased in the Church during their time). Among whom (as the most prominent) are held *Bede, Alcuin, John of Salisbury <note>original: "Ioannes Carnotensis"</note>, Giraldus, Nigellus, Neckam, Sewal, Baconthorpe, Ockham, Rolle of Hampole* and *FitzRalph of Armagh <note>original: "Armachanensis"</note>*: whose godly teachings and faithful judgments our *Wycliffe*, well-investigating and striving after, has confirmed and further declared. And among other of their writings, he held in very high esteem a certain book against the Religion of the Mendicant Orders <note>The "Begging Friars" such as the Franciscans and Dominicans</note>: published at that time by *Richard of Armagh*, Primate and Archbishop in Ireland, most learned in the Law and God's word, who was in great standing during the time of the one whom we present here and remember to the honor of God: being a Man truly of great understanding and exceptional learning: who at *Oxford* in *Merton* College, took up and mastered the Liberal Arts (as the learned call them) with singular diligence and bravery. Because of this, having shown the sharpness of his spirit and his then-esteemed ingenuity above others in Scholastic Theology <term>Scholastic Theology: A method of critical thought and teaching used in medieval universities that combined religious dogma with Aristotelian philosophy</term>, he was beheld with wonder for his judgments, good character, and learning. And having now been brought into prominence, he stepped before the eyes of <note>meaning: "confronted"</note> the powerful and numerous swarms of Mendicant Monks (with whom, according to many opinions, it was harder to clash than with the Pope himself), just as the aforementioned *Richard of Armagh* had done before. And not leaving it at that (as one who now, through the favor and goodwill of many, possessed more power), he has [attacked] the head itself <note>referring to the Pope</note>—from whose brains, like a Roman *Jupiter*, these foul, idle Mendicant Monks [sprang], [like] the bilge-water stench of all ships in displeasure...

<summary>This page introduces John Wycliffe as a foundational English reformer who built upon the intellectual legacy of earlier scholars to challenge the corruption of the Church and the influence of Mendicant orders.</summary>
<keywords>John Wycliffe, England, Reformation, Oxford, Merton College, Mendicant Orders, Richard FitzRalph of Armagh, Scholastic Theology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the biographical account of John Wycliffe, focusing on his theological challenges to the Papacy and the subsequent persecution he faced both during his life and after his death.</meta>

# JOHN <note>original: IOANNES, referring to John Wycliffe</note>

surpassing and overflowing) boldly attacked: and would not acknowledge nor allow the Pope to be such a Head of the Church: but declared him to be the true Antichrist. The Bulls, Indulgences, Masses, and their other theatrical plays <note>original: "Battement-spelen," a derogatory term for empty rituals or stage-plays</note>, and whatever else they mumbled without clear speech and under their breath, and had introduced outside of God's word, he rejected and refuted. That gruesome *Transubstantiation* <term>Transubstantiation: the Catholic doctrine that the bread and wine become the actual body and blood of Christ</term> or the Change of the bread into the Body of Christ, the magical Anointing, the executioner-like probing of consciences (or Auricular Confession), the vain and foolish works of Satisfaction, the first, second, and extreme Unction, together with all other superstitions or fabricated divine services, he rejected and despised alike. Briefly, he showed and confirmed that everything must be measured and judged according to the Rule of the Gospel, and not the Decrees or the Pope's institutions; according to the words of the Lord Christ, and not the words of the Pope. And because of this, he preached the Gospel with great fruitfulness under *Edward* III. Under which King's highly praised virtue *Wycliffe* stood secure: through whose power and prestige the Roman Pope at that time lost the right to choose Bishops, and the administration and power over the receipt of spiritual Tithes, such as Saint Peter's pence. Which *Polydore Vergil* <note>An Italian humanist and historian who wrote a history of England</note>, looking back on it later, took so indignantly: That he scolds *Wycliffe* in his Chronicle as a shameful and stubborn Heretic because of it. Therefore, we should not wonder that the Pope (as a follower of Peter the Fisherman) pursued the English Island's rich fishery with such zeal for the sake of the great income, and sought to regain it, and still seeks it. Under King *Richard* II, reigning since then, this pious man was found in exile, yet remaining steadfast in all things. Having recently returned from there, he passed away in England in the year of the birth of Christ, whom he preached, 1387, the last of December, and was buried in his Parish church at *Lutterworth*. Thereafter, in the year 1428, which was 42 years after his death, his bones—under Pope *Martin* V, following the *Council and the Decree of Siena*—were dug up and, as the remains of a gruesome and cursed person, burned to ashes. Thus, after his death, he experienced what the exceeding grimness of those was capable of, whom he had preached during his life to be cruel above all others. He has (according to the testimony of *Aeneas*

These are the books, indications, or names through which he most strongly attacks the Pope.

Besides these, he has written many books on Philosophy, and many through which he explains Holy Scripture, the Register of which can be seen in the *Centuries* of BALE <note>original: "BVLAEI" - referring to John Bale’s *Scriptorum Illustrium Maioris Britanniae... Catalogus*</note>: who, following the practice of Johannes Trithemius in the *Register of Ecclesiastical Writers*, has indicated the first words of the Prefaces, and almost all the Chapters of the books.

Wycliffe also translated the Bible into the English language, and added to each book his Preface and arguments.

He also translated the XII books of Clement, Priest of the Church of Llanthony, named *The Unity of the Evangelists*, with many treatises or interpretations of the ancient Doctors. And from this one can perceive **WYCLIFFE’S** Divine and rich-minded intellect, having produced so many books for the Church.

# ->JOHN WYCLIFFE, ENGLISHMAN.<-

<note>John Wycliffe (c. 1320s–1384) was an English philosopher and theologian whose followers were known as Lollards; he is often called the "Morning Star of the Reformation."</note>

> How great your Papal power and your raging hate,
> Your executioner-like murderousness, was in that time;
> The bones of the saints which you do not let rest,
> The same, to your shame, still reproaches you today.

> *The King of the Tonsured <note>original: "Gheſchoren" – referring to the shaved heads of monks and priests</note>, seeking only the earthly,*
> *Has filled the world with teachings of wicked cunning:*
> *But* Wycliffe *like God's Angel, seeking the pure heavenly things,*
> *Says to the English: Flee the contentious tonsured heap.*

### On the Cruelty and the Insanity of the Papists.

> Long after Wycliffe's bones had been buried,
> That cruel Papist folk consumed them with fire and flame,
> And through such a gruesome deed, like mad Papal slaves,
> (In scattering the ashes) the Christian people have been awakened.
<note>In 1428, 44 years after his death, Wycliffe's remains were exhumed, burned, and cast into the River Swift by order of the Council of Constance.</note>

<meta>This page continues the history of the early Reformers, transitioning from the influence of John Wycliffe in England to the rise of Jan Hus in Bohemia.</meta>

# JOHN HUS,
## A BOHEMIAN.

<image-desc>Large decorative initial 'O' set within an ornate square woodcut border featuring floral motifs.</image-desc>

Incredible indeed was the power and the might of the Gospel spread by the aforementioned *Wycliffe* in England. Whose strong growth and advancement neither the width of the intervening Ocean Sea, nor the far-stretching lands, were able to hinder, so that this mighty word did not fail to go and penetrate through the high mountains and the thick, dark forests (with which the Kingdom of *Bohemia* seems to be enclosed and encircled as if with walls). Behold there *JOHN HUS* OF *PRAGUE*, in the midst of the land of *Bohemia*, as well as the aforementioned capital city of the Kingdom and its widely renowned high school <note>The University of Prague, founded in 1348</note>, a preacher of this Gospel, strongly confirming the writings and propositions of the aforementioned *Wycliffe* and his already preached and increasing doctrine. Who, now being a Shepherd of the *Bethlehem* temple <note>The Bethlehem Chapel in Prague, where Hus preached in the Czech language</note>, in order to feed God's flock with the Heavenly bread, resolved to do so (not according to the Pope's Decrees or institutions—useless phlegm—nor according to the corrupt brains of the human head, the husks of outward works, nor even the decorative leaves of superstitions or fabricated religious services). For that is to practice well the office of a True *Bethlehem* Shepherd: namely, that he rightly divides the heavenly bread in God's house (whereby souls are nourished unto eternal life) and faithfully distributes it to Christ's sheep. This godly man, having been condemned as a heretic by Pope *Alexander* the fifth, appealed (through a public writing) to Christ, the true Judge. At that time, things stood very poorly with the Roman Church, when the Cardinals (to be well noted as a remarkable punishment from God in the changing of these ages), being contentious and divided among themselves, chose three different Popes; which the Kings (seeing the Roman Church in such a meager state and decline) took very badly. Therefore, some also sought a *Reformation* <term>Reformatie: a restoration or improvement of the church's conduct and doctrine</term> of the Church: *Pierre d'Ailly, Cardinal of Cambrai*, himself urging toward that end. In order to provide for this and to rectify Roman affairs, the Council at

<meta>catchword: Con- (likely referring to the Council of Constance)</meta>

<summary>This page describes how John Wycliffe's teachings spread from England to Bohemia, inspiring Jan Hus to preach the Gospel at the Bethlehem Chapel in Prague despite papal opposition. It highlights the "Western Schism" where three rival popes vied for power, leading to calls for church reform.</summary>

<keywords>Jan Hus, John Wycliffe, Bohemia, Prague, Bethlehem Chapel, Pope Alexander V, Pierre d'Ailly, Western Schism, Reformation, Church History</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the biographical accounts of early reformers, focusing here on the martyrdom of Jan Hus and the subsequent Hussite Wars.</meta>

# JOHN Hus

->*Constance* in Germany, in the year 1414, being established, *Jan Hus* was also called there, and upon the Emperor *Sigismund's* assurance and safe-conduct, he came to the aforementioned *Constance*: notwithstanding this, he was thrown into the dungeon: and the Emperor's assurance was violated and broken by that over-bold assembly. It was proposed and decided by the *Council*; *That no faith should be kept with Heretics*: <note>The Council of Constance (1414–1418) famously ruled that a promise of safe conduct to a heretic was not binding for the Church.</note> the Bohemians meanwhile protested the injustice of this in vain. Finally, these zealous fathers were ignited with such great and raging wrath, that they understood and judged that the aforementioned *Hus*, with all his books, must be thrown into the midst of the fire. But in you, *Hus*, the **Holy Spirit** at that time kindled and lit other fires. The Bishops desecrated you: and stripped you of the *Antichrist's* <note>A common Reformation-era term for the Papal vestments or the Roman Church hierarchy.</note> surcoat, you who had to climb to Heaven to *Christ* with that glorious garment of Faith. And as some of them approached you with a razor to skin the crown of your head, and others with shears to flay it, becoming quarrelsome over the matter; you (turning to the Emperor) said these words, not without joy:<-

> *Oh, little unity is there among the Bishops: and a wonder indeed it is, that while they are equally cruel together, they nonetheless do not agree in the cruelty itself.*

You, now wearing a paper crown upon your head, have many times surpassed in worth and blessedness the one who wears the triple crown of gold and precious stones in such pride. <note>A comparison between Hus's mocking paper "heretic's crown" and the Pope's triple tiara.</note> And so the highly gifted man was burned on the sixth of July of the following year. The ashes of him, *Hus* (or the Goose, as so named in the Bohemian language) <note>original: "Hus" means "Goose" in Czech.</note> foretelling that in his time he would bring forth a Swan in Saxony, from whose whiteness and the sound of its voice, those black Ravens and the unclean ones nesting in the high rocky crags—*Harpies* or fair-faced birds of prey who defile all things by touch—would be not a little dismayed and terrified. <note>This refers to Hus's famous prophecy: "Today you roast a goose, but in a hundred years a swan will come whom you will not be able to burn," later attributed to Martin Luther.</note> *Hus* being now taken up into Heaven by the wings of divine mercy and Faith, the Bohemians in those lands waged heavy but successful wars against the Roman *Cyclopes*, or one-eyed giants fighting against Heaven: who, beholding *Ziska's* <note>Jan Žižka, the famous blind Hussite general.</note> supreme Imperial Banner, displaying a Goose and a Chalice, shed their blood (defending the teaching of him who defended the Chalice as a true sign of the blood of *Christ* poured out for us).

Some remnants of a contrast between Christ and Antichrist.
History of the works of Christ, gathered together from the four Evangelists, and distinguished according to the three years of preaching.
History of the suffering of Christ, gathered from the four Evangelists and clarified through explanation.
Synodal Sermons, or sermons of the assembly.
Twenty-eight Sermons.
Explanation of the first seven Chapters of the first Letter to the Corinthians.
Seven explanations on the Canonical Letters of the Apostles.
Account of the 109th Psalm, and the nine following.
On the body of Christ in the Sacrament of the Altar; which is neither created nor begun, against the gross errors of the Papists.
On Adoration, and against the Adoration of Images.
Among these are some writings in defense of Johannes, Jerome of Prague, and John Wycliffe.

# JOHN HUS OF BOHEMIA

Wycliffe’s disciple, Hus, kept himself in the midst of the woods,
Just as Wycliffe did on the Island <note>England</note> which the Ocean surrounds.
Both praising Christ, which distressed the Tyrant of Souls <note>The Pope</note>,
And defending God’s word—through fire and water—as friends.

## On the Cruelty and
## Treachery of the Papists.

HUS and WYCLIFFE were both consumed by flaming fire:
The one shows the Pope’s Cruelty, the other his Treachery <note>Hus was burned alive at the Council of Constance despite a safe-conduct pass; Wycliffe's remains were later exhumed and burned.</note>,
Revealing what their Faith is, and the "mercy" of old Rome;
Now no glory <term>Roem</term> stands in Rome, but the fullness of wickedness.

### Against the Scroll of the Apostolic Chamber and the
### Roman Shop of Bulls.

"Anser" <note>Latin for Goose</note> is "Hus" according to the Bohemian language.
By Geese, Rome in ancient times remained free <note>A reference to the sacred geese of the Capitoline Hill who warned the Romans of a Gallic invasion in 390 BC.</note>.
Why is this Goose now against Rome? Her many failings
Are the cause; a Goose as angry as any goose-quill ever wrote.

# JEROME
## OF PRAGUE.

<image-desc>A large decorative initial letter "S" featuring intricate scrolled vine and floral patterns, set within a square border.</image-desc>

See here **Jerome of Prague’s** excellent countenance, forehead, eyes, and dignity; in short, in your whole being and appearance, you are, *Jerome*, according to the common judgment of the *Physiognomists* <note>Physiognomy was the historical practice of judging a person's character or intellectual capacity from their facial features.</note> (if one credits them), to be held as you have indeed shown yourself to be; through fine courage and virtue, he has proven himself, beloved viewer, to be a truly upright man.

For when he stood up for his fatherland, as well as for **John Hus**, who had been unworthily and by improper means seized and suppressed by many slanders, he traveled to *Constance*. Remaining quietly at *Überlingen*, which is an Imperial city located one mile from there, he requested public and safe conduct from the Emperor, but could obtain none. While he was everywhere making known and demonstrating the innocence of his fatherland, the aforementioned *Hus*, and his own defense against the godless accusers and slanderers, he was taken captive through certain traps laid for him along the way, and brought bound to the aforementioned *Constance*.

Here the accusers swarmed together like hornets and attacked *Jerome* with a great noise. *Gerson*, the Chancellor of the *Parisian* High School <note>The University of Paris (the Sorbonne).</note>, well known to everyone, brought up and reproached him in their midst for that which he had formerly said and proposed in *Paris* in a certain new manner; as one who, with his fine proofs and syllogisms, had set the Academy or High School there in an uproar. Likewise, a certain conceited theologian from *Cologne* presented to the slanderers the oration that this *Jerome* had delivered in former times at *Cologne*. And similarly, a *Master of Ours* <note>original: "Magister noster" — a formal academic title for a doctor of theology.</note> from *Heidelberg*, seated high in his chair, also vehemently attacked *Jerome*, bringing forth what he had observed and noted about him in *Heidelberg*, and so did many others.

And as *Jerome* was a man of mind and body, appearance, learning, virtue, and eloquence most select, and richly gifted with that which best becomes a man,

# JEROME

<columns>
so everyone watched him more closely and cast their eyes upon him. Therefore, such a great and excellent man was marked for certain death and beset by those pale-faced and fishy-eyed hunters of the Roman whores <note>original: "Roomſcher hoeren"; a common Reformation-era pejorative for the Roman Catholic Church</note>. Like *Hus* and *Wycliffe* before him, he cried out and railed much against the corrupt customs of the priests, their pride, greed, harlotry, and their unspeakable unchastity. Finally, he was condemned to the fire and brought to the place of the famous *Hus’s* burning: through the memory of which man the flame of godliness and virtue in his mind was all the more ignited and kindled. The strength of his spirit and his contempt for death were so wonderful and great in him that when he stood prepared at the stake, one could see that with a more righteous mind, he considered the extreme torments to be small and light. Thus, when the Executioner unexpectedly wanted to light the fire from behind, he said to him: *Bring the fire here, Executioner, and kindle it before my eyes: for if I had feared to see that, I would never have come to this place (which I could easily have avoided and fled).* The true manly virtue and *Constancy* <note>original: "Constantie"; referring to his steadfastness at the Council of Constance</note> of this holy *Jerome* was beheld with wonder by the entire city of *Constance*. Likewise, the bravery and steadfastness of this same man was expressed by *Poggius* <note>Poggio Bracciolini (1380–1459), an Italian humanist who witnessed the execution</note> (as an eyewitness to the same) through a great, very learned, and eloquent letter; of which *Aretinus* <note>Leonardo Bruni (c. 1370–1444), also known as Aretino</note> writes thus to *Poggius* in the fourth book of his Letters: *Three days ago we understood from the copy of your letter, sent to us by Barbara, concerning the final judgment of* JEROME: *the beauty of which letters I must rightly praise. Yet you seem to attribute more to him than I would wish to do: and although you often excuse your judgment, nevertheless I know not what greater inclination of spirit hides beneath it. I am of the opinion that one ought to write of this cautiously. Yet you,* Poggio, *need not be ashamed of that letter, nor let yourself repent of having written it, since therein is contained more virtue, soundness, and true learning than in five hundred of your kind of jest-books, which you have publicly poured out as the froth and filth of your spirit.*

Whether anything was written by Jerome of Prague and left to posterity is unknown to me.

<column-break/>

Yet it is believable that such a learned and eloquent man, especially in that darkness, [must] have [written] something excellent

# JEROME
## SAVONAROLA.

AFTER JEROME of PRAGUE follows here in due order *Jerome of Ferrara*. In names, in the pursuit of things, and in the subject matter they handled, both were holy. In magnanimity they were equal; in learning, in freedom of speech, in eloquence, and in steadfastness, they were alike in their affairs and in their manner of dying, both strictly punishing the godlessness, negligence, pride, unchastity, and especially the harlotry of the Popes and Clerics. But this man was a Monk of the Dominican order; the former <note>Jerome of Prague</note> had sworn to no laws of the hooded, shaven, or anointed <note>A derogatory reference to the tonsured and consecrated Catholic clergy</note>. Having now become a preacher, *Jerome of Savonarola* used and observed his preaching office and good gifts well, on account of which his prestige and influence among those of Florence was very great. As he was a sharp and strict rebuker and punisher of faults, he uncovered many coarse pieces and abuses among the clergy and the Roman Papacy, and publicly proclaiming the same, he foretold and promised great punishments, even the very uprooting of Rome. Explaining the Revelation of John <note>The Book of Revelation</note> in the year 1490, he applied and interpreted the greater part of it to the depicting of the Pope and the description of his downfall; he also taught many things in matters of Faith against the Pope and his Primacy or Supremacy. Furthermore, when Charles the Eighth, King of France, had undertaken his expedition through Italy to war against the Pope, the Emperor *Maximilian*, and the Venetians, *Jerome* often preached and foretold in his sermons that this same Charles was raised up and called by God to invade and destroy Italy: as a public rod over those who lived and drifted in all kinds of godlessness and the nature of unchastity, and also so that he might bring the clergy within bounds and measures, and punish the Tyrants of the Church. If not, he warned that God would punish Charles himself for his negligence. With such great vehemence was this zealous Preacher driven and broken out against the pride, unchastity, and coarse faults of the Priests.

D

-># JEROME<-
<note>original: "Hieronymus" - referring to Girolamo Savonarola</note>

Meanwhile, the Florentines remained devoted to **Jerome** and King **Charles**. It was observed that the King’s intention was to improve and restore the dominion and the organization of the Clerical Estate. *A laudable concept and intention it was of Charles, says Commines, but in the fulfillment of it lay much difficulty.* <note>Philippe de Commines (c. 1447–1511) was a writer and diplomat whose memoirs are a primary source for this period.</note> Now that matters have changed due to the King’s death, and the Florentines having fallen into heavy strife and discord among themselves, **Jerome’s** prestige and power among them diminished daily. And furthermore, the Pope incited the Florentines and promised many wonders if they would take his revenge and punishment upon **Jerome**. Many already hated him, especially a certain monk of the Franciscan order—the Franciscans generally being enemies to the Dominicans—who reviled **Jerome** as a heretic. Whereupon a certain commotion and riot of the common people occurred, which the new magistrates allowed by turning a blind eye; they seized him and inflicted great torments upon him. Finally, he was burned with two of his fellow brothers in the year <term>1498</term> <note>original: "m. cccc. xcviii."</note>. In the 55th year of his age, of which *Commines* writes thus: *In morals and manner of life he was irreproachable, as far as one could perceive in him; by teaching and instructing, he brought many here to understanding and the improvement of life.* The renowned historian *Guicciardini* felt similarly about *Savonarola*. After the aforementioned *Charles*, *Louis the Twelfth* received the Kingdom, and with the Kingdom, the will toward the destruction of Babylon, which his still-extant golden medals, with this inscription, testify: **Louis XII, by the Grace of God, King of France, Duke of Milan.** <note>original Latin: "LVD. XII. D. G. REX FRANC. DVX MEDIOL."</note> The meaning of which is, *Louis the 12th, by God’s grace King of France, Duke of Milan*, and on the other side of the medal **I will destroy Babylon**, <note>original Latin: "Perdam Babylonem"</note> which is as much as to say: *I shall bring Babylon down.* You were also fortunate for this reason, *Louis*, that you found *Philippe de Commines*—an exquisite historian for the sake of noble truth, great understanding, and judgment—as the proclaimer of your virtues; he who is also called a holy man by *Marsilio Ficino* and *Flaminio*, and was defended against the Pope by *Francesco Pico, Earl of Mirandola*, through an exquisite writing or booklet. This was when the Pope was *Alexander the Sixth, Borgia*. This Spaniard—out of ambition, no less than greed and shameful unchastity, a puffed-up and abandoned monster—acting of his own motion, and (as he says) out of the fullness of...

The *Meditation* was, for its singular godliness, honored by *Luther* <note>Martin Luther, the German Reformer</note> with a preface in print.

# DESIDERIUS ERASMUS
## OF ROTTERDAM.

<image-desc>A large decorative initial 'S' depicting a seated scholar or figure within the curves of the letter, surrounded by ornamental floral or scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>Let **DESIDERIUS ERASMUS** of **ROTTERDAM** also have his place here: his proper place, I say, if indeed he has one. The Papists <note>The Roman Catholic Church</note> do not recognize him as one of their own; the Evangelicals <note>Protestants</note> consider him partly theirs and partly a stranger. Do you ask, then, why *Erasmus* stands in this rank among those who openly and with all their might continually fought the Roman *Antichrist*? This shall serve as your reason and answer: Namely, that he was the one who exposed, rebuked, and contradicted the Scholastic Theology <note>The formal, often rigid academic theology of the Middle Ages</note> as a form of *Sophistry* <note>original: "Sophisterije" — deceptive or overly subtle reasoning</note>, or the hair-splitting of the Popes. He likewise challenged the hooded brothers <note>Monks and friars</note> and the idle superstitions, false beliefs, and fabricated religious services of many others in various ways, much to the delight of the common people. Indeed, he even showed many the way by which they could understand and uncover the lurking-holes, disguises, and hidden cunning of the supporters of the *Worldly Empire* <note>The political power of the Papacy</note>. Thus, he is regarded by the Papists as being equally quick or nimble with both hands, left and right <note>Meaning he was perceived as shifty or playing both sides</note>. Consequently, he became the intermediary who opened the window for the Evangelicals, through which they later flung open the great wide gates of Rome and let in their acquaintances in great multitudes.

This is exactly what a certain Spanish monk and preacher wanted to convince his fellow Spaniards of—those who held the garrison at Rotterdam (when the Duke of *Alba* was practicing his tyranny in the Netherlands). These soldiers had previously honored the statue of *Erasmus*, carved from blue stone and set high upon a certain bridge in the market as a place of distinction. However, moved by the shouting and denunciations of that same preacher, later that evening—around the time the Spaniards were accustomed to going on watch—these soldiers bravely fired their muskets and firearms at the statue of *Erasmus*. Attacking and overwhelming it with a great noise, they finally shoved it down from its height into the harbor. They did not wish to see any other statue of honor in the Netherlands above that of the aforementioned *Alba*, acting as spectators to the establishment of memorials to Spanish pride and cruelty...

# DESIDERIUS ERASMUS

nor have they exalted him. But the **Magistrates** (after the City was liberated from the tyranny of the Duke of *Alba* <note>Fernando Álvarez de Toledo, 3rd Duke of Alba, known for his harsh rule in the Netherlands during the Eighty Years' War.</note>) have erected another Statue to *Erasmus*: which truly is worthy to be among the city's foremost Ornaments and magnificent buildings, made of a better material; and through a different spirit, the art (for the sake of his excellent intellect, learning, and all kinds of arts) has been established and displayed. Yet the *Republic*; the luster and the praise obtained by all through *Erasmus of Rotterdam*, can extend and expand her Honor and Fame to a greater and more lasting degree than her own extensive shipping or notable Navigations today, reaching between both *Poles* and through the perishable new world. Besides this, for the sake of his Name, *Erasmus* is as much valued and beloved by us as we see today the sharp-witted and meddlesome monster of the *Jesuits* (according to the hatred they bear him) everywhere cutting through and scratching out the Name and writings of the same *Erasmus*: although he was one of the foremost from whom, as from an abundant fountain, good literature—now common among the learned—has strongly flowed, and these ungrateful slanderers have drawn and sucked it from him. As those who only intend and strive for this: that they might exalt themselves and cause themselves to be Praised above others throughout the whole world, and through such men as *Possevinus* <note>Antonio Possevino (1533–1611), a Jesuit diplomat and scholar who was a fierce critic of Erasmus.</note> and other boasters and know-it-alls, forcing themselves upon the **World** in place of the aforementioned *Erasmus*, hoping by that means to snatch an innumerable heap of books out of the hands of the lovers of his spirit, and especially his *Corrections, Revisions* <note>original: "Recognitien"</note> and *Scholia* or interpretations, which he produced through an incredible labor, greater than that of *Hercules*, over the entire works of *Jerome, Augustine, Ambrose, Cyprian, Hilary, Irenaeus*, and more other Latin *Fathers*: as also in the remaining works of *Origen*. So that they may thereafter thrust their *Corrections* and *Censures* or judgments into the hands of the inexperienced Buyer and make them more common. **For** we see how they defile with dirty hands, and contaminate with their fierce and sharp nails by scratching and tearing, the writings of the holy *Ancient Fathers*. And this under the cover that they are armed and provided with the Pope's *Bull-Privileges*, thus presuming over these Godly and Venerable *Fathers* everything they boldly and shamelessly desire and dare to undertake. These are the *Jesuits*, who, as the community well

# OF ROTTERDAM. 17

deeds, condemning everyone else, but wishing to be judged by no one themselves: those who derive their name from the Name of JESUS <note>The Jesuits or Society of Jesus</note>, thinking themselves holier and also wiser than true Christians. Just as the very learned man *Benedictus Arias Montanus* <note>A Spanish orientalist and editor of the Antwerp Polyglot Bible</note> gave such a judgment regarding these same Jesuits, not without sadness. Oh, if only you, great *Desiderius Erasmus of Rotterdam*, had been a steadfast and certain companion of the Evangelicals <note>Protestants</note>, and your wavering faith had not been noticed by your friends as well as your enemies! But you, looking as if to your own Name—which is said to mean Desire and Love—wished to be *Desiderius*, that is the "desirable one," and the other, *Erasmus*, that is the "lovely one." From this it followed that no one could say you were entirely or truly theirs: at times these, at times those calling you an enemy, and thus they have sufficiently made a mockery of you in matters of Religion (which is not to be mocked). Just as the sharp-witted Epigram or inscription by *Theodorus Beza* <note>A major French Protestant reformer</note>, standing here before your image, *Ingens Ingentem* etc. <note>original: "Ingens Ingentem quem personat orbis Erasmum" — "The great Erasmus, whom the great world echoes"</note>, feels or expresses his judgment. The learned shall please to expect the life of this great man through the favor of *Otto Werckmann of Nijmegen*, an intelligent man and our good friend: who preserves *Erasmus’s* life—never before issued in print and written by the same *Erasmus’s* own hand—as a precious jewel, and intends to bring it to light very shortly.

# DESIDERIUS ERASMUS

he has: to which are added prefaces that he has written or noted down for all kinds of writers.

**IV.** Comprising everything that pertains to the adoption and maintenance of good morals: translated from Plutarch <note>Plutarch (c. 46–120 AD), a Greek philosopher and biographer</note>, books of *Apophthegmata* <note>original: "Apophthegmata"; short, pithy sayings or maxims</note> or short sententious words, *The Praise of Folly*, the instruction of a Christian Prince dedicated to Charles V, Isocrates' <note>An ancient Greek rhetorician</note> little books on the instruction of a Prince translated into Latin <note>Erasmus translated these from Greek into Latin to make them accessible to Western scholars</note>, Declamations or practice orations on death, for a Bishop, *The Complaint of Peace*, *The Tongue*, and several others.

**V.** *Enchiridion* <note>original: "Enchiridion"; literally "handbook" or "dagger"</note> or the *Handbook of the Christian Soldier*, narrations and expositions on some Psalms, Consolations, Prayers, the institution of Christian marriage, *The Preacher*, and various theological treatises.

**VI.** Comprises the New Testament, now for the fifth and last time very diligently revised and corrected, with fine annotations or notes.

**VII.** *Paraphrasis* <note>original: "Paraphrasis"; a retelling of the text to make the meaning clearer</note> or extended, elegant discourses on the entire New Testament.

**VIII.** Theological or Divine writings translated into Latin from the books of some Greek theologians, namely from Chrysostom, Athanasius, Origen, and Basil.

**IX.** Comprises the defenses against those who have slandered him—either with insufficient respect or with malice—concerning certain passages in his books,

<column-break/>

as the inscription or the preface contains.

There are many defenses in this ninth volume; had Erasmus refrained from writing them, the man’s name might have been held in even higher esteem <note>The translator suggests Erasmus's reputation suffered from his frequent, sometimes bitter, public arguments with critics</note>.

Now, if anyone should wish to leisurely review and read the authors—both spiritual and secular—whom Erasmus has corrected (which are nearly countless), they would be able to perceive his learning, care, vigils, immense labor, and the excellence of his spirit, not without wonder and great notice.

Now, regarding the censors, correctors, or examiners of his books: these are those who, with the indications of their corrections, improvements, or purifications, piteously haul Erasmus over the coals, tormenting and martyring him. And although we see many errors and clearly marked deletions in Erasmus's writings through their scrutiny and cross-examination, there are nonetheless many that they have blindly passed over, as not a few people are able to notice.

The *Indices* <note>original: "Indices"; refers to the Index Librorum Prohibitorum or lists of prohibited/expurgated books</note> or pointers for the purification of these fine books are two; one of which was published in the Netherlands in the year [15]71 by the command and authority of the Catholic King <note>Philip II of Spain, who ruled the Netherlands at the time</note>, as
